# The cutscene list

Every cutscene of the game, read from its files by `tools/cutscene_list.py` (roadmap phase 1, for decision
15: cutscenes skippable, shortened or removed). **Made by the tool: do not edit this file by hand,** run
`python tools/cutscene_list.py "<romfs>"` again. The same list as data is `data/cutscenes.json`.
`tests/cutscene_list_check.py` reads the game's files again and fails when either file is out of date.

## What is in it

| | |
|---|---|
| Events in the game (`RSDB/EventSetting`) | 2380 |
| Event flow files (`Event/EventFlow`) | 901, 480 of them cutscene files (`Dm_...`, `DmF_...`) |
| **Story cutscenes** (events named `Dm_...`) | **319** |
| Lead-in events (not `Dm_`, they run a story cutscene as a step) | 217 |
| Small scenes (not `Dm_`, placed in a map: a door opens, a chest appears, Navi speaks) | 363 |
| System scenes (`Dm_SY_...`: getting an item, opening a chest, the ocarina, changing map) | 79 |
| Cutscene files that belong to one of these events | 473 of 480 (the rest: "Cutscene files without an event" below) |

## How to read an entry

- **Kind.** *timeline*: a fixed film (camera, animations, voices on a time track; files `<name>`,
  `<name>_Effect`, `<name>_Env`). *movie*: a video file (`Event/Movie/<name>.webm`), with a timeline beside it
  for sound. *flowchart*: a list of steps (walk here, say this, wait, give that).
- **Length.** A timeline's length is in its file, in frames; **60 frames are one second** (12 movie
  cutscenes have a timeline beside their video file, and for 11 of them it is as long as the video to
  within 2%: e.g. `Dm_Kokiri_0095`, 7040 frames, video 117.33 s; the exception is `Dm_FinalBattle_0002`,
  1732 frames, video 33.0 s). A flowchart has no length: "waits" is the time of its wait steps on its longest
  path, **at 60 frames a second, which is not checked in game for flowcharts**; animations, camera moves and
  the player reading text come on top. So for a flowchart it is the least it can take, not its length.
- **Sets / gives.** Only a flowchart can set a flag or give an item: no timeline in the game has a step
  that does (the 261 timeline files hold camera, animation, sound, light and effect steps; the one thing
  a few of them set is the time of day). What a timeline cutscene "sets" is set by the event that runs it,
  listed under **Started by** and under the area's lead-in events. Flags are GameData names; "challenge"
  is the game's quest step (`RSDB/Challenge`). "On its EventFinish the logic sets" = the map's logic
  graph sets it when the event ends, not the event itself.
- **Played.** The game also remembers for every event that it has been played (`HavePlayedEvent.<event>`).
  "Played is tested by" names the logic graphs and flows that ask for it: removing or skipping such a
  cutscene has to keep that answer true.
- **Can be skipped.** "no" = the event's settings list has `DisableSkip`. Everything else can be skipped
  by the game's own skip (not tried for each one in game).
- **Started by.** Another event (a transition step), or an actor in a map. For an `EventRequestActor`
  the condition is read from the logic graph of its group; `Area_xxxx`, `EventRequestActor_xxxx` are the
  names of that graph's actors.

### Lead-in events (10)

Not cutscene files themselves: they run a story cutscene as one of their steps. What they set before
and after it is what the cutscene "sets".

**`Get_StoneOfFire_ArriveFromKokiri`** (flow `Dm_SY_BancChangeArrive`, entry `Get_StoneOfFire_ArriveFromKokiri`; waits 0.9 s)

Not cutscene files themselves: they run a story cutscene as one of their steps. What they set before
and after it is what the cutscene "sets".

### Lead-in events (8)

Not cutscene files themselves: they run a story cutscene as one of their steps. What they set before
and after it is what the cutscene "sets".

## System scenes

`Dm_SY_...` events: the game's own pieces for getting an item, opening a chest, the ocarina, changing map,
game over. They are run as steps of other events, not placed in maps.

## Event names on other map actors

Besides `RequestEventName` and `InitEventName` (the scenes above), map actors name events in these
parameters for their own work (a door's opening, a chest's get scene, a Navi hint, a scrub's talk).
They are not listed one by one.
